P N GADGIL JEWELLERS LIMITED
ANNUAL REPORT 2025-26


===== PDF PAGE 3 =====
P N Gadgil Jewellers (PNG Jewellers) is one of India’s oldest jewellery brands, 
built on a remarkable 194-year legacy of trust, craftsmanship, and enduring 
customer relationships. Deeply rooted in Maharashtra’s cultural heritage, the 
brand has been a part of generations of celebrations, earning a reputation for 
purity, design excellence, and customer-centricity.
For nearly two centuries, this legacy has evolved alongside changing consumer 
aspirations, cultural traditions, and jewellery preferences. While remaining 
anchored in the values that have defined it for generations, the brand has 
continuously embraced to remain a trusted choice across generations in an 
ever-changing market.
Today, PNG Jewellers stands at the intersection of heritage and opportunity. 
Its expanding presence across geographies reflects the enduring appeal of 
a brand built on trust, quality, and authenticity. From traditional jewellery to 
modern collections, the brand continues to connect with a diverse and evolving 
customer base.
is a tribute to the foundation that shaped the brand and the opportunities that lie 
ahead. What began in Sangli has evolved into a C10,000 crore jewellery brand, 
steadily expanding its footprint across the nation while remaining true to the 
values that define us.
It reflects the enduring values that have guided PNG Jewellers since 1832, 
alongside its growing presence across India. Deeply connected to its origins, 
the brand continues to honor the trust, craftsmanship, and relationships that 
have defined generations while reaching new markets, new customers, and 
new possibilities.
As PNG Jewellers enters the next chapter of its journey, it remains committed 
to the integrity, quality, and customer-centricity that have always defined 
the brand. 
Rooted in Legacy.  
Rising Nationwide.
ROOTED IN LEGACY.
RISING NATIONWIDE. 
A Legacy that Endures.  
A Journey that Continues!

Who We Are
A LEGACY CRAFTED 
IN GOLD
At PNG Jewellers, jewellery is more than adornment — it is a 
celebration of heritage, a reflection of Indian craftsmanship, and 
an expression of individuality. Since 1832, this philosophy has 
guided our commitment to quality, trust, and timeless design.
Vision
To build on our legacy since 1832 and evolve from our leadership in Maharashtra into a nationally admired 
jewellery brand - trusted and cherished across generations, relevant to a changing India, and recognised as an 
organisation of choice for customers, partners and talent.
Mission
To bring PNG closer to customers across India and select international markets through a strong store 
network and seamless omnichannel presence; to deliver exceptional jewellery and experiences through 
craftsmanship, innovation and technology; and to build a high-performing organisation where the best talent 
thrives with pride and purpose, creating lasting value for all stakeholders.
Values 
Tradition
Since its inception, PNG Jewellers has kept traditions at 
the centre of its craftsmanship and business. This practice 
continues even today as we now carry traditions with a touch of 
modernity. Our designs bring heritage and trends together thus 
giving our customers the best of both. We take pride in keeping 
the tradition alive through our products and the relations we 
foster with our customers.
Trust
Our first and utmost important Value, PNG Jewellers stands tall 
on Trust. It’s not just a word; it is the building block of the brand. 
From the metal to transactions everything at PNG Jewellers is 
done keeping trust of customers at priority. At PNG, trust binds 
us with our customers at the outside and with our employees 
at the inside. We believe in operating as one big family bringing 
all our employees together with our practices and ethics and 
standing together at all times.
Legacy
Established in 1832, PNG Jewellers 
carries a legacy that is enriching. We 
proudly carry the legacy of being the 
torch-bearer of traditions and carry 
forward craftsmanship that honors the 
rich heritage of our country.
Promise
PNG Promise is synonymous to an 
unbreakable bond. Be it in terms of 
service, product or purity, we strive to 
keep our promise towards customers.
From a single store in Sangli to one of India’s most 
respected jewellery brands, we have built a legacy that 
blends tradition with contemporary sophistication. As of 
March 31, 2026, we operate 78 retail stores, including 
77 in India and 1 in the United States. Our retail footprint 
spans five states in India, providing a strong foundation 
for our next phase of growth as we advance our vision of 
becoming a truly Pan-India jewellery brand. 
Our portfolio comprises diverse collections across gold, 
silver, diamond and platinum jewellery, catering to a wide 
range of occasions. It also includes YOOU by PNG, our 
dedicated sub-brand catering to the growing demand 
for contemporary, lightweight, and design-led jewellery 
among modern consumers.
As a leading player in the organized jewellery retail 
segment, our strong brand recall is anchored in 
uncompromising quality, transparent practices, and an 
exceptional customer experience — delivered seamlessly 
through our integrated network of showrooms and 
digital platforms.

our culture, our metals are pure and 
our values are purer. Transparency in 
transactions and our offerings is utmost 
priority for us.

===== PDF PAGE 8 =====
The brand's positioning
Lightweight diamond  
jewellery that takes  
fashion seriously
captures its core proposition of offering stylish, 
versatile, and wearable jewellery without 
compromising on craftsmanship or authenticity.
As the jewellery market continues to witness 
trends such as premiumization, self-purchase, 
and lifestyle-led consumption, YOOU enhances 
our portfolio architecture and strengthens our 
ability to address evolving customer needs. 
Through this strategic brand evolution, we 
are creating a platform for long-term growth 
while ensuring continued relevance across 
generations of consumers.
YOOU is built on  
four key strategic pillars
REIMAGINING  
EVERYDAY JEWELLERY: 
INTRODUCING YOOU BY PNG JEWELLERS
We rebranded LiteStyle as YOOU by PNG Jewellers, a lightweight 
diamond jewellery brand created to strengthen our connection 
with younger, style-conscious consumers and address the growing 
demand for lightweight designs across 9, 14, 18 and 22 karat gold.
Launched to bridge the gap between traditional jewellery and contemporary everyday wear, YOOU 
specializes in lightweight diamond jewellery as consumer preferences have evolved towards more 
expressive, design-led pieces, thus creating a brand that resonates more deeply with today’s 
women. The transition to YOOU reflects this evolution, placing the wearer at the centre of every 
design philosophy while retaining the quality, purity and craftsmanship that customers have come to 
associate with PNG Jewellers.
The transition from LiteStyle to YOOU reflects our commitment to building 
a more distinctive and emotionally resonant brand identity. As consumer 
preferences evolve beyond occasion-led purchases towards self-
expression and lifestyle-driven consumption, YOOU has been designed 
to cater to these emerging aspirations while retaining the trust and 
credibility of PNG Jewellers.
BEFORE | LITESTYLE
• Often misconstrued as “Lifestyle” 
• Generic, category-led name with 
limited differentiation 
• Lacked a distinctive and ownable 
brand identity 
• Higher investment requirements 
AFTER | YOOU
• Built around Attitude — a distinctive brand territory 
• Distinctive, memorable and easy to own 
• Puts the consumer at the centre of the 
brand proposition 
• Higher stud contribution to the overall mix 
• Leaner investment with potential for stronger returns 
An identity built around a 
feature rather than a distinctive 
consumer proposition
Interlocking “O”s echo the parent PNG 
identity, creating a link between heritage 
and youthful expression
Design-First Positioning
Contemporary, fashion-forward jewellery 
that combines lightweight comfort with 
premium aesthetics.
Engaging New  
Consumer Cohorts
Targeted at younger professionals and 
modern consumers who view jewellery as an 
extension of their personality and lifestyle.
Celebrating Everyday 
Moments
Moving beyond traditional occasions to 
serve consumers seeking jewellery for work, 
social gatherings, and daily self-expression.
Trust of PNG Jewellers
Leveraging nearly two centuries of heritage, 
craftsmanship, and quality assurance 
while adopting a fresh and progressive 
brand identity.

===== PDF PAGE 9 =====
 
Favorable Industry Tailwinds: 
Organized Retail Shift & Rising 
Disposable Incomes
• Strong growth outlook for the Indian jewellery 
market, projected to reach USD 215.2 billion 
by 2030, with CAGR of 20% over FY25–30P—
providing a long runway for organized players 
like PNG
• Structural shift from unorganized to organized 
retail: organized players are growing meaningfully 
faster than the overall industry, with their market 
share rising from ~32% in FY 2020 to ~39% 
in FY 2025, and projected to reach ~45% by 
FY 2030E
• Gems and jewellery sector contribute 
approximately ~7% to India’s GDP 
• Growing aspiration among younger 
demographics for lightweight, design-led gold 
and diamond jewellery as a lifestyle statement, 
not just an investment
 
Leadership in Maharashtra with 
Accelerating Pan-India Store 
Expansion
• Dominant retail presence anchored in 
Maharashtra—one of India’s largest jewellery 
consumption markets—with a growing footprint 
spanning across 5 states in India and 1 in the 
United States
• Network of 78 stores across 36 cities as of 
March 31, 2026, comprising 57 COCO and 21 
FOCO outlets
• 25 new stores opened in FY26, the fastest 
expansion in the company’s history
• Entered high-potential new states—Madhya 
Pradesh, Uttar Pradesh, and Bihar—in FY26, 
broadening the addressable market significantly
• Expansion into Tier 2 and Tier 3 markets 
positions PNG to capture rising per capita 
jewellery spend beyond metros
• Balanced COCO–FOCO expansion model and 
asset-light franchise-led growth strategy enable 
capital-efficient national scaling
Investment Case
WHAT SETS US APART 
PNG offers a differentiated investment opportunity anchored in 
heritage-led trust, leadership in Maharashtra—India’s second-
largest jewellery consumption state—scalable retail economics, 
accelerating premiumization, and strong financial execution. With 
₹1,07 ,391 million in revenue, 87 .8% PAT growth, and 78 stores 
across 36 cities as of FY2025–26, PNG is positioned for sustained, 
profitable growth in India’s structurally expanding organized 
jewellery industry.

Strong Financial Performance, 
Operating Leverage & Credit Profile
• Revenue grew 39.6% YoY to C1,07,391 million 
in FY2025–26, crossing the C10,000 Crore 
milestone for the first time; four-year revenue 
CAGR of ~33.6% 
• EBITDA expanded 89.6% YoY to C7 ,040 million, 
with EBITDA margin improving from 4.8% (FY25) 
to 6.6% (FY26)
• PAT grew 87 .8% YoY to C4,098 million; PAT margin 
expanded from 2.8% to 3.8%; EPS increased 
to C30.20 in FY26 from C17.10 in FY25
• ROCE improved significantly to 30.5% in 
FY26 from 20.2% in FY25, and ROE of 20.9% 
demonstrates high-quality, capital-efficient 
earnings generation
• Inventory Turnover Ratio of 3.8× highlights robust 
working-capital productivity
• Company’s Long-Term Rating upgraded  for 
its Bank Loan Facilities to IND A+/Stable 
and affirmed the Short-Term Rating at IND 
A1 reflecting superior financial health and 
creditor confidence

Premiumization & Emerging High-
Growth Segments
• Average Transaction Value of C1,00,225, 
reflecting premiumization and increasing 
consumer willingness to spend
• Stud Ratio of 9.9% in FY26, with targeted efforts 
to grow this higher-margin category—expected to 
rise further with expansion into Northern states
• E-commerce segment delivered ~ 105% YoY 
growth, validating the digital channel strategy
• Digital footprint expanded across Zepto, Swiggy 
Instamart, Blinkit, Tata CLiQ, Amazon, AJIO, 
Flipkart, and Myntra—driving new customer 
acquisition and incremental revenue
• YOOU by PNG—targeting modern women 
addresses the fast-growing demand for 
9kt/14kt/18kt/22kt lightweight, design-led, 
everyday jewellery
• New stores in Northern and Central India already 
show a higher stud ratio than the established 
Maharashtra–Goa network, a mix advantage set 
to scale as expansion continues
 
Superior In-Store Experience & Store-
Level Productivity
• Premium in-store experience across 2,37 ,903 
sq. ft. of total retail area, with a standardized 
store design language and service philosophy 
consistently delivered across all 78 locations
• Average revenue per store of C1,376.8 million, 
underpinned by high footfall, strong conversion, 
and superior in-store customer experience
• Same-Store Sales Growth of 42.8% in FY26, 
highlighting strong organic store momentum
• Trained workforce of 2,950 employees, with 
79,949 training hours logged in FY26 covering 
product expertise, quality standards, and 
customer engagement
• Order-to-Made conversion of 37 .2%, reflecting 
strong customer intent and high-quality 
engagement at the point of sale
• Value-added in-store services including same-
day URD (old-gold exchange) transactions, repair 
works, and made-to-order (MTO) jewellery
 
Deep Consumer Loyalty & Repeat 
Engagement
• Multi-generational brand affinity built over 194 
years, with PNG Jewellers embedded as a 
preferred brand across life’s key milestones—
from first jewellery purchases to weddings 
and inheritance
• Gold savings and purchase schemes driving 
disciplined repeat engagement; average upsell 
of 75% over accumulated scheme value upon 
redemption in FY2025–26
• Structured loyalty program rewarding 
frequent buyers, deepening customer lifecycle 
engagement and reducing churn in a high-
consideration, low-frequency category
• 26.91% New customers added Y-o-Y
• Served customer base of over 3 million
• 91% Conversion Ratio
 
Design-Led Product Portfolio with 
Rigorous Quality Assurance
• Portfolio of 42,819 SKUs spanning gold, silver, 
diamond, and platinum across 16 collections and 
1 sub-brand (YOOU by PNG), catering to diverse 
occasions, price points, and aesthetics
• 100% of gold jewellery is BIS hallmarked and 
all diamond jewellery is laboratory certified—an 
early adopter of BIS standards, reinforcing a 
reputation built on purity and transparency
• Design philosophy blends traditional Indian 
craftsmanship with contemporary aesthetics—
exemplified by collections such as Tulika (inspired 
by Uttar Pradesh’s Kundan and filigree heritage) 
and The Golden Katha, crafted from century-old 
Gokak Karagiri molds
• Rigorous quality control protocols embedded 
across the full value chain, from bullion and 
diamond sourcing to finished product, supported 
by 181 active supplier partnerships
• Strong ecosystem of 112 supplier vendors 
across geographies and 69 exclusive karigars 
crafting unique designs — with the top 3 karigars 
associated with PNG since 1971 — preserving 
heritage craftsmanship while supporting 
scalable, consistent production across a rapidly 
expanding store network
 
Strong Corporate Governance 
Anchored in Ethical Sourcing & Board
• Responsible and 100% ethical gold sourcing 
through traceable channels like RBI nominated 
banks, recycled gold
• Strong board diversity with 3 Women Directors 
on the Board, including 2 Women Independent 
Directors, reflecting a commitment to inclusive 
and independent governance
 
Heritage-Led Brand with 194 Years of 
Consumer Trust
• Founded in 1832 in Sangli, PNG Jewellers is 
among India’s oldest and most trusted jewellery 
brands, with a 194-year legacy spanning six 
generations of the Gadgil family
• Deep-rooted customer relationships embedded 
across wedding, festive, and everyday jewellery 
occasions, underpinned by multi-generational 
household loyalty
• Recognized among India’s Top 10 Best Work 
Places in Retail for the third consecutive year, 
reflecting a strong institutional culture
• National brand visibility reinforced by celebrity 
ambassadors Madhuri Dixit and Ranbir Kapoor 
for the mainline brand, and Sara Tendulkar 
for YOOU by PNG, targeting the modern and 
millennial segment

SCALING NEW MARKETS
We have built a strong and steadily expanding retail network, 
anchored by a deep presence across Maharashtra—our core 
market—while strategically strengthening our footprint across 
key cities in Central and North India. As of March 31, 2026, 
our network comprises 78 stores across 36 cities, including 57 
company-owned (COCO) and 21 franchise-owned (FOCO) outlets.
Presence
Franchising remains a key pillar of our growth 
strategy, enabling us to expand our presence 
across existing and emerging markets. Through a 
scalable and asset-light expansion model, we are 
extending the reach of the PNG Jewellers brand, 
delivering a consistent customer experience, and 
supporting sustainable long-term growth.
MR. AMIT VAIDYA 
Head of Franchisee Development & Operations

CHAIRMAN & 
MANAGING DIRECTOR’S 
MESSAGE 
From Sangli in 1832 to ₹1,07,391 million in  
FY26  this journey reflects not only the growth 
of PNG Jewellers but also the aspirations and 
progress of generations of customers whose 
milestones we have adorned.
Dear Stakeholders,
It gives me immense pride to write to you at the close of a 
landmark year for your Company. FY 2025-26 was a year 
in which PNG Jewellers crossed the C10,000 crore revenue 
milestone — a moment that belongs not to any one year, but to 
the 194 year journey of trust that six generations of our family, 
our karigars, our employees, and above all, our customers have 
built together. When a legacy this old delivers growth this young, 
it reaffirms our conviction that heritage and ambition are not 
opposites — they are partners.
A year of profitable, high-quality growth
Your Company’s revenue from operations grew 39.6% to 
C1,07,391 million. What makes this performance particularly 
satisfying is not its scale alone, but its quality. Gross margin 
expanded by 280 basis points to 12.0%, EBITDA expanded 
by 180 basis points to 6.6%, and profit after tax grew 87.8% 
to C4,098 million. This was the outcome of exiting low-margin 
refinery sales, enriching our product mix with studded and 
silver jewellery, and scaling our lightweight diamond sub-brand, 
YOOU. Silver, in particular, was a standout, growing 131.8% 
during the year, while our diamond and studded portfolio 
continued to gain share.
Equally encouraging was the performance of our newer engines 
of growth. Our franchisee channel grew 83.0% and e-commerce 
more than doubled, growing 105.2%. These channels embody 
the twin principles that guide our expansion — capital efficiency 
and customer proximity.
Expanding our footprint,  
deepening our presence
As of March 31, 2026, your Company operated 78 stores 
— 57 Company-owned and 21 franchise-operated — across 
Maharashtra, Goa, Madhya Pradesh, Uttar Pradesh and Bihar, 
together with our store in the United States, spanning over 2.37 
lakh sq. ft. of retail space. Our asset-light FOCO model is allowing 
us to take the PNG brand to newer geographies faster, without 
compromising the discipline of our balance sheet. Behind this 
expansion stand our people: we welcomed more than 850 
new colleagues during the year and continued to invest in their 
training and growth.
YOOU: A brand for a new generation
A special word on our lightweight diamond jewellery business, 
which represents one of the most exciting chapters in our 
growth story. Through the year, this business — then operating 
under the LiteStyle identity — was an important contributor 
to our improving product mix and margin profile, bringing 
accessible, everyday diamond jewellery, crafted across 9, 14, 
18 and 22 karat gold, to a customer who wears jewellery not 
merely for occasions, but as an expression of who she is. Shortly 
after the close of the year, on August 8, 2026, we rebranded 
LiteStyle as YOOU — a name that embodies individuality, 
confidence and self-expression — and opened its first flagship 
store at Amanora Mall, Pune. From its present footprint of 
exclusive outlets and shop-in-shop counters, we aspire to take 
YOOU to 100 stores across India by 2030. In YOOU, nearly 
two centuries of PNG’s trust and craftsmanship meet the 
individuality of young India — and we believe this combination 
gives us a genuine right to win in the fast-growing lightweight 
diamond jewellery segment.
An industry moving our way
The operating environment, while not without challenges, 
continues to shift structurally in favor of organized, trusted 
jewellers. Mandatory hallmarking, rising digital adoption, 
the preferences of a younger generation of consumers for 
branded, design-led and lightweight jewellery, and the steady 
formalisation of the Indian economy are all accelerating the 
movement from unorganized players to brands like ours. 
At the same time, we remain watchful. Geopolitical tensions 
have introduced volatility in commodity prices and supply 
chains, and the revision of the effective import duty on gold to 
15% has implications for input costs across the industry. Your 
Company navigates these realities with the same discipline that 
has defined it for generations — prudent inventory management, 
agility in responding to market movements, and a robust risk 
management framework.
Strengthening our capital base
Shortly after the close of the year, in August 2026, your 
Company successfully completed a Qualified Institutions 
Placement (QIP), raising C700 crore. The strong institutional 
participation in the issue is a powerful endorsement of your 
Company’s strategy and growth trajectory. The proceeds will 
strengthen our balance sheet and provide the capital foundation 
for the next phase of our store expansion beyond our core 
markets, in line with the objects set out in the placement 
document — allowing us to grow with ambition while preserving 
the financial discipline that has defined this institution.
Looking ahead
Our path forward is clear. We will deepen our leadership in our 
core markets while expanding into high-growth geographies 
through a calibrated mix of Company-owned and franchise 
stores, with our network expected to grow to 100+ stores by 
FY27, 140+ by FY28, and 170+ by FY29. Indeed, in the coming 
year, we look forward to a moment of great pride for every 
member of the PNG family: the opening of our 100th store, a 
milestone that once seemed a distant dream for a business that 
began on a pavement in Sangli.
We will continue to enrich our product mix — growing studded, 
silver and lightweight jewellery — and scale YOOU as our bridge 
to the aspirations of modern women and millennial India. And 
through it all, we will remain uncompromising on the values 
that have carried this institution for nearly two centuries: purity, 
transparency, and craftsmanship.
Gratitude
On behalf of the Board of Directors, I thank our customers for 
their unwavering trust, our employees and karigars whose skill 
and dedication bring our jewellery to life, our franchise partners 
for growing with us, our bankers and business associates for 
their support, and you, our shareholders, for your confidence 
in our journey. The milestones of FY26 are a foundation, not a 
destination. We look to the future with humility for what we have 
inherited and ambition for what we can build.
With warm regards,
DR. SAURABH VIDYADHAR GADGIL
Chairman & Managing Director 
P N Gadgil Jewellers Limited

Operating Environment 
TRENDS THAT SHAPE US
Multiple structural and consumer-led trends are reshaping India’s 
jewellery market and supporting the growth of trusted, organized 
brands. Rising preference for branded jewellery, demand for 
contemporary yet culturally rooted designs, and wider access 
through organized retail and digital channels have strengthened 
our competitive position.
The organized jewellery segment continues 
to outperform the broader market, driven by 
regulatory tailwinds, quality assurance, and 
transparency—key differentiators in a trust-
led category. 
• Organized players growing faster than the 
overall industry — projected ~23.5% CAGR vs. 
~20% for the total market (FY 2025–30) 
• Market share increased from ~32% (FY 2020) 
to ~39% (FY 2025), projected to reach ~45% by 
FY 2030E
Our Approach 
• Adopted stringent quality controls and 
hallmarking standards
• Benefited from mandatory BIS hallmarking, 
favoring organized retailers
• Elevated in-store experience through improved 
design, service, and engagement
• Enabled seamless purchasing across physical 
and digital platforms
Shift from Unorganized to Organized Retail
Jewellery demand in India is strongly influenced 
by festivals, weddings, and agricultural income 
cycles, with peak periods during the year.
Our Approach 
• Aligned inventory planning and design launches 
with festive and wedding calendars
• Introduced exclusive collections for key festivals 
and occasions
• Ensured optimal stock availability during peak 
demand periods
• Strengthened marketing and digital 
engagement to maximize visibility
• Effectively captured demand across urban 
centres and emerging rural markets
Seasonal and Regional Demand Drivers
  Source: https://www.tribuneindia.com/news/business/organized-jewellery-players-set-to-capture-45-market-share-by-fy30-nomura/ 
Jewellery has been integral to India’s cultural, social, 
and economic fabric for centuries. Today, the gems and 
jewellery sector is among the largest contributors to the 
Indian economy and plays a meaningful role in exports, 
employment, and value creation.
• Market size of USD 86.4 billion in FY 2025, projected to 
reach USD 215.2 billion by FY 2030 
• CAGR of ~20.0% (FY 2025–30); grew at ~ 13.5% CAGR over 
FY 2020-25 
• ~7% contribution to India’s GDP (domestic retail 
contributing 2–3% points of this) 
• Gems & jewellery exports of USD 27 .7 billion in FY 2026, 
projected to reach USD 75 billion by FY 2030 
• Among India’s largest foreign exchange earners; sector 
supports millions of skilled and unskilled jobs across 
mining, polishing, design, and retail, with over 5,00,000 
local goldsmiths and jewellers in the unorganized 
segment alone
Our Approach 
• Leveraged a long-standing legacy 
to reinforce trust amid gold 
price volatility
• Expanded offerings across gold, 
silver, platinum, and diamonds to 
serve diverse budgets and occasions
• Strengthened customer stickiness 
through structured savings and 
instalment schemes
• Promoted lightweight jewellery, 
old-gold exchange, and flexible 
purchase options
• Accelerated digital adoption 
via owned platforms and 
online marketplaces
• Expanded physical presence into 
underpenetrated non-metro markets
Rising incomes and changing mindsets 
are accelerating premiumization. 
Consumers increasingly favor branded, 
design-led jewellery that offers certified 
quality, superior craftsmanship, and a 
differentiated experience.
Our Approach
• Enhanced store ambience, service standards, and 
product presentation
• Curated premium collections and customized designs 
across occasions
• Maintained transparent pricing and billing to reinforce trust
• Strengthened brand positioning as a national, organized 
jewellery retailer
Indian Jewellery Market: Scale and Significance
Jewellery consumption is increasingly 
driven by younger, style-conscious 
consumers. Demand is shifting toward 
lightweight, contemporary designs 
suitable for everyday wear, with 
jewellery seen as a lifestyle statement 
rather than only an investment.
Our Approach 
• Launch of YOOU – lightweight diamond brand to cater the 
modern women and millennials
• Developed offerings tailored to new-generation  
consumers
• Focused on contemporary, lightweight designs for 
everyday use
• Balanced affordability, durability, and aesthetic appeal
Evolving Consumer Preferences
Premiumization: A Structural Growth Driver
  Source: The Knowledge Company Industry Report

Customer Experience 
TRUST AT THE CORE OF OUR 
CUSTOMER PROPOSITION
Driven by design excellence and seamless technology integration, 
PNG Jewellers continues to serve customers across all age 
groups and geographies in India. A sustained focus on quality, 
transparency, and customer-centricity has helped build enduring 
trust across generations.
Value-Added Customer Benefits
PNG Jewellers offers a range of gold savings and purchase schemes that enable customers to plan jewellery 
purchases over time while availing benefits on gold, diamond jewellery, and making charges. These schemes 
enhance affordability, encourage disciplined savings, and strengthen customer engagement. They also serve as 
an effective customer retention and upselling platform, driving repeat purchases. 
These schemes continue to be a key contributor to customer retention and upselling, with an average upsell of 
75% over the accumulated scheme value upon redemption in FY26.
Hallmarking and Purity 
Assurance
PNG Jewellers adopted hallmarking practices 
over 15 years ago and is widely associated with 
purity and authenticity. All diamond jewellery 
is laboratory certified, reinforcing the brand’s 
commitment to quality, transparency, and trust.
Speed-to-Market through 
Streamlined Operations
Standardized, end-to-end processes across 
design, procurement, quality control, and 
inventory management ensure consistent 
product availability and enable rapid store 
scaling. This integrated operating model 
enhances efficiency, maintains uniform quality 
standards, and supports disciplined expansion 
across geographies.
Future Purchase 
Plan  (FPP)

Plan (DPP)
Golden Gain Plan 
(GGP)
Suvarna Laxmi Labh 
Plan (SLLP)
Technology-Led Customer Experience
The e-commerce business delivered strong growth momentum during the year, with the segment 
growing by ~ 105% YoY, significantly strengthening scale and category contribution.
During the year, we expanded our digital footprint across major quick-commerce platforms, including 
Zepto, Swiggy Instamart, and Blinkit, while strengthening our presence across leading digital marketplaces 
such as Tata CLiQ, Amazon, AJIO, Flipkart, and Myntra. Key technology initiatives included migration to 
Shopify, launch of the mobile application, and implementation of systems for order management, logistics 
optimization, and automated inventory and pricing updates. These initiatives strengthened operational 
efficiency, reduced turnaround times, and improved customer experience.

& convenience

product portfolio with value-added savings 
schemes, bespoke made-to-order offerings, and 
an enhanced customer experience, we continue to 
deepen customer engagement, strengthen brand 
loyalty, and drive sustainable growth.

Marketing and Brand Building Strategies
CRAFTING A BRAND  
FOR GENERATIONS 
PNG Jewellers continues to strengthen its brand leadership 
through a comprehensive marketing strategy that combines 
targeted advertising, celebrity-led brand building, and a rapidly 
expanding digital ecosystem. By leveraging multiple consumer 
touchpoints across traditional, digital, and e-commerce channels, 
the Company enhances brand visibility, deepens customer 
engagement, and drives sustainable growth across existing and 
new markets. 
Advertisement & Reach
Hyperlocal marketing model focused on store-level 
customer engagement and footfall generation. 
Exhibitions and store-specific campaigns drive 
awareness and footfalls in surrounding catchment areas.
Event-led campaigns centred around key occasions 
such as Dussehra, Diwali, Akshaya Tritiya, and wedding 
collection launches. 
Integrated mix of outdoor, print, TV, OTT, digital, and 
social media channels maximize reach and recall. 
Branding & Buzz
Celebrity-led campaigns featuring Madhuri Dixit and Ranbir 
Kapoor enhance brand visibility and consumer connect. 
Sara Tendulkar fronts YOOU by PNG, strengthening appeal 
among younger and modern consumers. 
Strategic associations with cinema fraternity help build 
visibility across regional and national markets. 
Pre-launch marketing and celebrity-led launch events create 
strong buzz for new store openings and market entries.
Successful Market Expansion: Strengthened brand 
presence through 25 store launches, including 11 YOOU by 
PNG stores and 14 legacy stores, supporting deeper market 
penetration and wider consumer reach.
Our marketing and e-commerce strategy is focused 
on strengthening brand visibility, deepening customer 
engagement, and expanding our digital reach. Through 
targeted campaigns, data-driven insights, and continued 
investments in our digital capabilities, we are building a 
strong foundation to unlock future growth opportunities 
and enhance customer accessibility. At the heart of 
this is our CRM initiative, which brings us closer to our 
customers, engages with them more meaningfully, and 
keeps that sense of family alive at every touchpoint.

People 
ENGAGING WITH CARE 
Our people are the cornerstone of our progress. The Company’s 
growth and long-term success are driven by the commitment, 
capability, and judgment of our workforce. 
We strive to build an organization where employees are trusted to make sound business decisions, 
supported by a culture that is open, safe, inclusive, and energizing. By nurturing talent through continuous 
learning and meaningful opportunities, we are shaping a future-ready organisation focused  
on innovation, value creation, and thought leadership.
Our people are at the heart of PNG Jewellers’ 
growth. We are committed to building an 
inclusive, empowering, and future-ready 
workplace, supported by continuous learning, 
strong engagement, and a focus on well-being. 
Our recognition as one of India’s Top 10 Work

our teams to deliver excellence and drive 
sustained success.
MRS. PRIYANKA VANJARI

capabilities. At PNG Jewellers, we adopt a multi-

employee growth.
New employees participate in a structured induction

values, product portfolio, and service philosophy. 
This is complemented by technical training in product

engagement, and collaboration—enhancing both 
workplace culture and customer experience.

Governance
ACCOUNTABILITY DRIVES 
PERFORMANCE 
Our practices extend beyond regulatory compliance 
to foster trust, strengthen risk management, and 
enable sustainable performance. Clear role definition 
and proactive stakeholder engagement support 
disciplined decision-making and prudent risk 
management. By integrating ESG principles into 
our governance framework, we align sustainability 
with strategy—enhancing resilience, reinforcing 
stakeholder confidence, and driving responsible, 
inclusive growth.
At PNG Jewellers, corporate governance is a cornerstone of our 
commitment to ethical conduct, transparency, and long-term value 
creation. Our robust governance framework is designed to uphold 
the highest standards of integrity, accountability, and fairness 
across the organization.
Board of Directors
The Board of Directors is central to our governance 
framework and providing strategic direction. 
Comprising members with diverse and relevant 
expertise, the Board enables informed, transparent, 
and balanced decision-making. It oversees critical 
areas including risk management, financial integrity, 
and regulatory compliance, reinforcing a strong 
culture of ethics and accountability. Through this 
stewardship, the Board strengthens stakeholder trust 
and supports inclusive, sustainable value creation.
Diverse Expertise 
The Board of PNG Jewellers brings together a wealth 
of knowledge, perspective, professionalism, divergent 
thinking and experience. Our Board Members have 
a deep understanding of governance, technical, 
financial and non-financial issues.

Management Discussion and Analysis
1. Company Overview 
  P N Gadgil Jewellers Limited (PNG Jewellers) is a 
leading jewellery retailer with a legacy of 194 years. The 
Company offers a diversified portfolio of gold, diamond, 
platinum, and silver jewellery, complemented by YOOU, 
its contemporary lightweight diamond jewellery sub-
brand, catering to a wide range of customer segments 
and occasions.
  As of March 31, 2026, PNG Jewellers operated 78 retail 
outlets, comprising 57 COCO stores and 21 FOCO 
stores across Maharashtra, Goa, Madhya Pradesh, 
Uttar Pradesh, Bihar and including one store in the 
United States, spanning a total retail area of 2,37,903 
sq. ft. Supported by a strong brand heritage, and 
customer-centric approach, the Company continues 
to strengthen its position in India’s organised jewellery 
market under the leadership of Dr. Saurabh Vidyadhar 
Gadgil, a sixth-generation entrepreneur with over 26 
years of industry experience.
2. Economic Review
2.1 Global Economy
  The global economy remained resilient in CY 2025, 
supported by steady private consumption, sustained 
investment activity across major economies, 
accommodative financial conditions, and rising 
investments in artificial intelligence and advanced 
technologies. Although economic growth in the United 
States moderated during the latter part of the year, 
partly due to temporary government disruptions, overall 
activity remained stronger than expected. Continued 
strength in technology-led business investment largely 
offset weakness in other investment categories and the 
impact of trade-related uncertainties.
  However, the global economic environment has become 
increasingly complex in CY 2026 following the outbreak 
of conflict in the Middle East. The conflict has added 
to existing geopolitical and economic uncertainties, 
resulting in higher commodity prices, disruptions to key 
trade routes, and increased volatility across financial 
markets. These developments have also contributed 
to elevated inflation expectations and heightened risk 
aversion among businesses and investors.

 Outlook 
  According to the International Monetary Fund (IMF), 
global growth is projected at 3.1% in CY 2026 and 3.2% 
in CY 2027, assuming the geopolitical conflict remains 
contained. While the global economy has demonstrated 
resilience, growth is expected to remain below recent 
trends and well below pre-pandemic averages. Rising 
commodity prices, persistent inflationary pressures, 
and tighter financial conditions are likely to weigh on 
economic activity.
  The challenges are expected to be more pronounced 
in emerging and developing economies, particularly 
commodity-importing nations with existing 
macroeconomic vulnerabilities. Furthermore, an 
escalation of geopolitical conflicts, increasing 
fragmentation of global trade and supply chains, 
slower-than-expected productivity gains from artificial 
intelligence, or renewed trade tensions could pose 
additional downside risks to the global growth outlook.
2.2 India
  India continued to be among the fastest-growing 
major economies, recording 7.7% growth in FY2025–
26, supported by resilient domestic demand, robust 
consumption and sustained public and private 
investment. Growth remained broad-based, with 
continued momentum across services, agriculture 
and industrial activity, alongside improving urban and 
rural demand.
  Strong government spending on infrastructure, energy 
and industrial development continued to strengthen 
long-term economic prospects. Large-scale initiatives 
across railways, defence and infrastructure, including 
the National Infrastructure Pipeline and manufacturing 
incentive programmes, supported activity across 
engineering, power and industrial sectors.
  The economy also benefited from improving 
manufacturing capacity utilisation, stronger financial and 
corporate balance sheets, increasing formalisation and 
continued policy reforms, including GST rationalisation. 
Together with India’s favourable demographics and 
expanding infrastructure base, these factors are 
expected to support sustained medium-term growth.

 Outlook
  India’s economic outlook for FY2026–27 remains

strong macroeconomic fundamentals and continued 
investment in infrastructure and manufacturing. 
Ongoing fiscal, monetary, labour and trade reforms are 
expected to reinforce economic activity and sustain 
growth momentum.
  An improving fiscal position and foreign exchange 
reserves of over USD 690 billion provide a strong 
cushion against external uncertainties, including 
geopolitical tensions and global market volatility. While 
risks from trade disruptions, energy price movements 
and capital flows persist, India’s diversified growth 
drivers, policy stability and expanding investment 
ecosystem are expected to support its position among 
the world’s fastest-growing major economies over the 
medium term.
3. Industry Review 
3.1 Global Jewellery Industry
  According to Fortune Business Insights, the global 
jewellery market was valued at USD 242.79 billion in 
2025 and is projected to grow to USD 387.36 billion 
by 2034, registering a CAGR of 5.41% during the 
forecast period. The industry continues to benefit from 
rising consumer spending on discretionary and luxury 
products, supported by growing disposable incomes, 
evolving fashion preferences, and increasing demand 
for premium lifestyle products. Asia Pacific remained 
the largest market, accounting for 39.23% of the global 
jewellery market in 2025.
  Demand growth is being driven by increasing consumer 
preference for contemporary and design-led jewellery, 
particularly among younger demographics seeking 
products that reflect personal style and self-expression. 
Jewellery continues to serve not only as an adornment 
but also as a symbol of status, wealth preservation, 
and cultural significance. The growing influence of 
fashion trends, expanding retail networks, and the 
entry of innovative designers are further contributing to 
market expansion.
 Key Market Drivers
 Rising Disposable Incomes and Premiumisation
  Growing disposable incomes, increasing purchasing 
power, and rising demand for luxury products are 
driving jewellery consumption globally. Demand is 
further supported by evolving consumer preferences, 
greater workforce participation among women, and 
the continued cultural significance of jewellery across 
key markets.
  Digital Influence and Retail Expansion
  Digital media, fashion trends, and social platforms are 
increasing consumer interest in contemporary and 
branded jewellery. Simultaneously, expanding retail 
networks, omnichannel strategies, and new brand 
launches are improving accessibility and supporting 
market growth.
 Outlook 
  The industry’s long-term outlook remains favourable, 
supported by rising urbanisation, increasing affluence 
across emerging economies, and growing adoption 
of branded jewellery. In addition, the continued shift 
towards organised retail and digital commerce is 
enhancing consumer access and driving demand 
across global markets.
3.2 India Jewellery Industry 
  The Indian jewellery market continues to be driven by its 
strong cultural significance while adapting to evolving 
consumer preferences. While weddings and festivals 
remain key demand drivers, consumers are increasingly 
seeking lightweight, contemporary, and personalised 
designs. Rising digital adoption, growing preference for 
branded jewellery, and changing lifestyle aspirations—
particularly among younger consumers—are reshaping 
purchasing patterns and supporting the growth of 
organised players.
  India’s gem and jewellery exports remained resilient in 
FY 2025–26 despite geopolitical uncertainties, evolving 
global trade dynamics, and tariff-related challenges. 
Total exports stood at ₹2,44,827 crore, registering a 
growth of 0.93% in rupee terms, while exports in dollar 
terms were recorded at US$ 27.72 billion.
  The year reflected a structural shift in the industry, with 
exporters increasingly diversifying into new markets 
and strengthening value-added product segments. 
While exports to the US remained subdued due to 
tariff-related uncertainties, markets such as the UAE, 
Australia, Hong Kong, and Canada delivered strong 
growth. Free Trade Agreements (FTAs) with the UAE and 
Australia played a significant role in supporting export 
diversification, with anticipated FTAs with the UK and 
EU expected to provide further momentum.
 Key performance trends 
 •  Cut and polished diamonds remained the largest 
export category, contributing 43.9% of total 
exports, though demand was impacted by tariff 
increases, inventory corrections, and subdued 
discretionary spending.
 •  Studded gold jewellery exports grew by 6.27%, 
reflecting strong demand for value-added and 
design-led products despite elevated gold prices.
 •  Silver jewellery exports surged by 52.21%, driven 
by strong global demand, rising silver prices, and 
increasing affordability.
 •  Platinum jewellery exports increased by 39.32%, 
highlighting growing acceptance in premium 
jewellery segments.
Management Discussion and Analysis
 •  Gold jewellery studded with lab-grown 
diamonds recorded robust growth of 31.30%, 
indicating evolving consumer preferences and 
product innovation.
 •  Lab-grown diamond exports  witnessed price-
led moderation despite higher volumes, reflecting 
ongoing market correction.
 Policy Updates
  The Union Budget 2026–27 introduced several 
measures aimed at strengthening the gems and 
jewellery sector. Key initiatives include customs reforms 
focused on trust-based processes, digital appraisals, 
and faster clearances to improve efficiency and reduce 
transaction costs. The removal of the ₹10 lakh cap on 
courier exports is expected to provide a significant 
boost to e-commerce-led exports, enabling MSMEs, 
artisans, and jewellery brands to access global markets 
more effectively. Additionally, investments in design 
capabilities, including the establishment of a new 
National Institute of Design, are expected to foster 
innovation, enhance value addition, and strengthen the 
sector’s global competitiveness.
  During the year, the effective import duty on gold 
was revised to 15%, comprising 10% Basic Customs 
Duty (BCD) and 5% Agriculture Infrastructure and 
Development Cess (AIDC), compared with the 
earlier effective duty of 6%. The change represents 
an important development for the domestic gems 
and jewellery industry, with implications for input 
costs and the broader operating environment for 
jewellery businesses.

 Outlook
  The Indian gem and jewellery industry is increasingly 
transitioning towards a more diversified and resilient 
export ecosystem. Growing demand for value-
added jewellery, expanding opportunities under free 
trade agreements, rising acceptance of alternative 
materials such as lab-grown diamonds and platinum, 
and continued market diversification are expected to 
support long-term growth. India’s strong manufacturing 
capabilities, skilled workforce, and established position 
in the global jewellery value chain continue to reinforce 
its competitiveness in international markets.

4. SWOT Analysis
 Strengths
 •  Established and well-recognised jewellery brand 
with a growing Pan-India presence, supported by 
strong customer trust, loyalty, and heritage.
 •  Diverse product portfolio spanning multiple price 
points, including ready-to-wear collections and 
made-to-order jewellery, supported by regular 
introduction of new designs aligned with evolving 
consumer preferences. 
 •  Robust distribution network with wide geographic 
coverage across multiple sales channels, enabling 
strong market reach. 
 •  Presence across digital platforms, including 
the Company’s own e-commerce website and 
leading online marketplaces and quick commerce 
sites, enabling broader customer access 
across geographies.
 Weaknesses
 •  Significant concentration of revenue in western 
India, particularly Maharashtra, exposing 
the Company to regional economic and 
demand fluctuations. 
 •  High dependence on gold jewellery, resulting 
in limited product diversification and exposure 
to volatility in consumer preferences and gold 
demand cycles. 
 •  High reliance on physical retail operations, 
leading to exposure to rental costs and store-level 
operating expenses.

 49

 Opportunities
 •  Expansion potential beyond core markets in 
Maharashtra into other high-growth regions across 
India, supporting stronger nationwide presence. 
 •  Increasing consumer preference for lightweight 
and studded jewellery, offering scope to enhance 
product mix and improve margins. 
 •  Growing adoption of digital and multi-channel 
retailing, enabling improved customer engagement 
and conversion across online and offline platforms. 
 •  Rising penetration of organised jewellery retail in 
India, supported by a shift from unorganised to 
branded players. 
 •  Opportunity to expand international presence by 
tapping demand from the global Indian diaspora 
and select overseas markets. 
 •  Asset-light expansion through franchise-based 
models, enabling faster store rollout with efficient 
capital deployment. 
 Threats
 •  Geopolitical tensions and ongoing conflicts, 
particularly in key regions, leading to supply chain 
disruptions, higher logistics costs, and uncertainty 
in input availability.
 •  Volatility in global gold and commodity prices, 
impacting demand, pricing, and inventory valuation. 
 •  Changes in government regulations, including 
import duties, compliance requirements, and 
taxation policies, affecting operating costs 
and margins. 
 •  Highly competitive industry structure, with 
participation from organised retailers, unorganised 
players, and online platforms, leading to pricing 
pressure and market share competition. 
5. Financial Performance

  FY 2025-26 was a milestone year for the Company, with 
revenue crossing the ₹10,000 crore mark. Importantly, 
growth was accompanied by a meaningful improvement 
in the quality of earnings: gross margin expanded by 280 
bps to 12.0% and EBITDA margin by 180 bps to 6.6%, 
driven by a richer product mix, the discontinuation of 
low-margin refinery sales, and strong traction in the 
franchisee and e-commerce channels. Profit after tax 
grew 87.8% to ₹4,098.2 million.
 Financial & Operational Performance
 Revenue from Operations
  Revenue from operations increased by 39.6% year-on-
year to ₹107,391.0 million in FY26 from ₹76,934.7 million in 
FY25. The strong growth was driven by healthy demand 
across the retail segment, along with robust growth in 
the franchisee and e-commerce channels. Retail revenue 
grew by 50.5%, while the franchisee and e-commerce 
segments recorded strong growth of 83.0% and 105.2%, 
respectively. The Company’s performance was further 
supported by an improved product mix, led by a higher 
contribution from studded jewellery.
 Cost of Goods Sold
  COGS as a percentage of revenue improved to 87.9% in 
FY26 from 90.8% in FY25, reflecting an enhancement in 
the Company’s gross margin profile. The improvement 
was primarily driven by the discontinuation of low-
margin refinery sales, a stronger contribution from 
silver and studded jewellery, which supported overall 
margin improvement.
 Employee Expenses
  Employee expenses increased to ₹1,571.96 million 
in FY26 from ₹1,121.33 million in FY25, reflecting the 
Company’s continued investment in human capital to 
support business growth. The increase was primarily 
driven by the addition of more than 850 employees 
during the year, in line with the expansion of the store 
network and overall scale of operations. Continued 
focus on workforce development, employee training, 
and capability-building initiatives also contributed to the 
higher employee cost.
 Finance Costs
  Finance costs increased to ₹915.79 million in FY26 from 
₹434.53 million in FY25, primarily due to higher working 
capital requirements arising from the Company’s 
expanding scale of operations. The increase was 
primarily due to bank interest expenses and interest on 
lease liabilities, reflecting the continued expansion of 
the store network.
 Depreciation
  Depreciation expenses increased to ₹571.87 million in 
FY26 from ₹348.38 million in FY25, reflecting continued 
investments in new showrooms, store renovations, 
technology infrastructure, and other fixed assets 
undertaken to support long-term growth.
 Other Expenses
  Other expenses increased to ₹5,290.27 million in FY26 
from ₹2,561.78 million in FY25, primarily driven by 
Management Discussion and Analysis
higher business promotion and customer acquisition 
initiatives. The increase was largely attributable to 
higher promotional and marketing expenditure to 
support expansion in new territories. These investments 
were undertaken to support sales growth, enhance 
brand visibility, and strengthen customer engagement 
across markets.
 Product performance
 •  Gold: Gold sales increased to ₹96,652.76 million in 
FY26 from ₹71,035.39 million in FY25, registering 
a strong 36.1% year-on-year growth. The growth 
was driven by robust consumer demand, strong 
festive and wedding season sales, successful 
promotional campaigns, and the benefit of higher 
gold prices during the year. 
 •  Silver: Silver sales rose significantly to ₹6,104.95 
million in FY26 from ₹2,633.99 million in FY25, 
reflecting an impressive 131.8% growth. The 
increase was supported by strong demand for 
silver products, expansion of the silver jewellery 
portfolio, and enhanced customer engagement 
initiatives, resulting in a higher contribution from 
the category.
 •  Diamond & Others: Revenue from diamond 
jewellery and other product categories increased 
to ₹4,597.47 million in FY26 from ₹3,233.86 million 
in FY25, representing a 42.2% year-on-year 
growth. The performance was driven by increasing 
consumer preference for diamond jewellery, 
supported by a broader product offering and 
growing demand across customer segments.
  Note – The ‘Others’ segment includes Platinum, Precious Stones, 
and articles sold on MRP.

6. Risk Management
  The Company has implemented a structured risk 
management framework designed to proactively identify, 
evaluate, and mitigate potential risks, thereby reducing 
both their probability of occurrence and overall impact. The 
Board of Directors provides oversight of this framework 
through periodic reviews of the Risk Management 
Committee’s activities, ensuring strong governance and 
continuous strengthening of risk controls.
Sr. 
No. Risk Area Mitigation Approach
1 Geographic 
Concentration 
Risk
The Company is undertaking strategic 
initiatives to expand its store presence 
beyond Maharashtra, with the objective of 
diversifying its geographical footprint and 
reducing concentration risk.
2 Cyber 
Security Risk
Implementation of a comprehensive cyber 
security framework to safeguard against 
digital threats.
3 Gold Price 
Volatility
Use of hedging strategies including Gold 
Metal Loan (GML), derivatives, and URD 
to manage price fluctuations.
4 Consumption 
Risk
Continuous monitoring of consumer 
trends through merchandising and 
marketing teams, supported by sales 
feedback and ground-level insights.
5 Regulatory 
and 
Compliance 
Risk
Ongoing tracking of changes in 
regulations, taxation, and import duties 
to ensure timely compliance and risk 
mitigation.
7. Business Outlook
  The Company intends to deepen its presence in its core 
markets while expanding into high-growth geographies 
through a calibrated mix of Company-owned (COCO) and 
asset-light franchise (FOCO) stores. Continued scaling 
of the YOOU sub-brand, growing contribution from the 
franchisee and e-commerce channels, and an improving 
share of studded and silver jewellery are expected to 
support both revenue growth and margin resilience 
over the medium term. The Company will continue to 
prioritise disciplined working capital management and 
investments in brand, design, and digital capabilities.
8. Internal Control
  The Company has established a strong internal control 
system aimed at ensuring operational efficiency, accuracy 
in financial reporting, and adherence to applicable 
laws and regulations. The control environment includes 
segregation of duties, defined approval hierarchies, 
proper documentation practices, safeguarding of 
assets, and regular performance monitoring. The 
Internal Auditor, who reports directly to the Audit 
Committee, conducts periodic audits and recommends 
corrective and preventive actions where required. These 
internal controls are regularly reviewed and refined to 
remain aligned with the Company’s scale and evolving 
operational requirements.
9. Cautionary Statement
  This Management Discussion and Analysis contains 
certain forward-looking statements relating to the 
Company’s objectives, projections, estimates, 
expectations, or forecasts, which are subject to risks 
and uncertainties. Actual results may differ materially 
from those expressed or implied in such statements. Key 
factors that may influence actual performance include 
availability and cost of raw materials, demand and 
pricing conditions in key markets, changes in regulatory 
and taxation frameworks, fluctuations in foreign 
exchange rates, particularly US$/₹, macroeconomic 
conditions in India and other relevant geographies, and 
other external factors impacting business operations.

How do I vote electronically using NSDL e-Voting 
system?
The way to vote electronically on NSDL e-Voting system 
consists of “Two Steps” which are mentioned below:
Step 1: Access to NSDL e-Voting system
A)  Login method for e-Voting and joining virtual 
meeting for Individual shareholders holding 
securities in demat mode
  In terms of SEBI circular dated December 9, 2020 
on e-Voting facility provided by Listed Companies, 
Individual shareholders holding securities in demat 
mode are allowed to vote through their demat 
account maintained with Depositories and Depository 
Participants. Shareholders are advised to update their 
mobile number and email Id in their demat accounts in 
order to access e-Voting facility.
 Login method for Individual shareholders holding securities in demat mode is given below:
Type of shareholders  Login Method
Individual Shareholders 
holding securities in 
demat mode with NSDL
1.  For OTP based login you can click on https://eservices.nsdl.com/SecureWeb/evoting/evotinglogin.jsp. You 
will have to enter your 8-digit DP ID,8-digit Client Id, PAN No., Verification code and generate OTP . Enter the 
OTP received on registered email id/mobile number and click on login. After successful authentication, you 
will be redirected to NSDL Depository site wherein you can see e-Voting page. Click on company name or 
e-Voting service provider i.e. NSDL and you will be redirected to e-Voting website of NSDL for casting your 
vote during the remote e-Voting period or joining virtual meeting & voting during the meeting.
2.  Existing IDeAS user can visit the e-Services website of NSDL Viz. https://eservices.nsdl.com either on a 
Personal Computer or on a mobile. On the e-Services home page click on the “Beneficial Owner” icon 
under “Login” which is available under ‘IDeAS’ section , this will prompt you to enter your existing User 
ID and Password. After successful authentication, you will be able to see e-Voting services under Value 
added services. Click on “Access to e-Voting” under e-Voting services and you will be able to see e-Voting 
page. Click on company name or e-Voting service provider i.e. NSDL and you will be re-directed to e-Voting 
website of NSDL for casting your vote during the remote e-Voting period or joining virtual meeting & voting 
during the meeting.
3.  If you are not registered for IDeAS e-Services, option to register is available at https://eservices.nsdl.com.  Select 
“Register Online for IDeAS Portal” or click at https://eservices.nsdl.com/SecureWeb/IdeasDirectReg.jsp 
4.  Visit the e-Voting website of NSDL. Open web browser by typing the following URL: https://www.evoting.
nsdl.com/ either on a Personal Computer or on a mobile. Once the home page of e-Voting system is 
launched, click on the icon “Login” which is available under ‘Shareholder/Member’ section. A new screen 
will open. You will have to enter your User ID (i.e. your sixteen digit demat account number hold with NSDL), 
Password/OTP and a Verification Code as shown on the screen. After successful authentication, you will be 
redirected to NSDL Depository site wherein you can see e-Voting page. Click on company name or e-Voting 
service provider i.e. NSDL and you will be redirected to e-Voting website of NSDL for casting your vote 
during the remote e-Voting period or joining virtual meeting & voting during the meeting.
5.  Shareholders/Members can also download NSDL Mobile App “NSDL Speede” facility by scanning the QR 
code mentioned below for seamless voting experience.
      
Individual Shareholders 
holding securities in 
demat mode with CDSL
1.  Users who have opted for CDSL Easi / Easiest facility, can login through their existing user id and password. 
Option will be made available to reach e-Voting page without any further authentication. The users to login 
Easi /Easiest are requested to visit CDSL website www.cdslindia.com and click on login icon & New System 
Myeasi Tab and then user your existing my easi username & password.
2.  After successful login the Easi / Easiest user will be able to see the e-Voting option for eligible companies 
where the evoting is in progress as per the information provided by company. On clicking the evoting 
option, the user will be able to see e-Voting page of the e-Voting service provider for casting your vote 
during the remote e-Voting period or joining virtual meeting & voting during the meeting. Additionally, there 
is also links provided to access the system of all e-Voting Service Providers, so that the user can visit the 
e-Voting service providers’ website directly.
3.  If the user is not registered for Easi/Easiest, option to register is available at CDSL website www.cdslindia.
com and click on login & New System Myeasi Tab and then click on registration option.
4.  Alternatively, the user can directly access e-Voting page by providing Demat Account Number and PAN 
No. from a e-Voting link available on www.cdslindia.com home page. The system will authenticate the 
user by sending OTP on registered Mobile & Email as recorded in the Demat Account. After successful 
authentication, user will be able to see the e-Voting option where the evoting is in progress and also able to 
directly access the system of all e-Voting Service Providers.

password’.

Step 2: Cast your vote electronically and join 
General Meeting on NSDL e-Voting system
How to cast your vote electronically and join General 
Meeting on NSDL e-Voting system?
1.  After successful login at Step 1, you will be able to see 
all the companies “EVEN” in which you are holding 
shares and whose voting cycle and General Meeting is 
in active status.
2.  Select “EVEN” of company for which you wish to 
cast your vote during the remote e-Voting period 
and casting your vote during the General Meeting. 
For joining virtual meeting, you need to click on “VC/
OAVM” link placed under “Join Meeting”.
3.  Now you are ready for e-Voting as the Voting 
page opens.
4.  Cast your vote by selecting appropriate options i.e. 
assent or dissent, verify/modify the number of shares 
for which you wish to cast your vote and click on 
“Submit” and also “Confirm” when prompted.
5.  Upon confirmation, the message “Vote cast 
successfully” will be displayed. 
6.  You can also take the printout of the votes cast by you 
by clicking on the print option on the confirmation page.
7.  Once you confirm your vote on the resolution, you will 
not be allowed to modify your vote.
General Guidelines for shareholders
1.  Institutional shareholders (i.e. other than individuals, 
HUF, NRI etc.) are required to send scanned copy (PDF/
JPG Format) of the relevant Board Resolution/ Authority 
letter etc. with attested specimen signature of the duly 
authorized signatory(ies) who are authorized to vote, 
to the Scrutinizer by e-mail to csabhijitdesai@gmail.
com with a copy marked to secretarial@pngadgil.com 
and evoting@nsdl.com. Institutional shareholders (i.e. 
other than individuals, HUF, NRI etc.) can also upload 
their Board Resolution / Power of Attorney / Authority 
Letter etc. by clicking on “Upload Board Resolution 
/ Authority Letter” displayed under “e-Voting” tab in 
their login.
2.  It is strongly recommended not to share your password 
with any other person and take utmost care to keep your 
password confidential. Login to the e-voting website 
will be disabled upon five unsuccessful attempts to key 
in the correct password. In such an event, you will need 
to go through the “Forgot User Details/Password?” or 
“Physical User Reset Password?” option available on 
www.evoting.nsdl.com to reset the password. 
3.  In case of any queries, you may refer the Frequently 
Asked Questions (FAQs) for Shareholders and e-voting 
user manual for Shareholders available at the download 
section of www.evoting.nsdl.com or call on.: 022 - 4886 
7000 or send a request to Sagar S. Gudhate, Assistant 
Vice President at evoting@nsdl.com .
Process for those shareholders whose email ids are 
not registered with the depositories for procuring 
user id and password and registration of e mail ids 
for e-voting for the resolutions set out in this notice:
1.  In case shares are held in physical mode please 
provide Folio No., Name of shareholder, scanned copy 
of the share certificate (front and back), PAN (self-
attested scanned copy of PAN card), AADHAAR (self-
attested scanned copy of Aadhaar Card) by email to 
secretarial@pngadgil.com . 
2.  In case shares are held in demat mode, please provide 
DPID-CLID (16-digit DPID + CLID or 16 digit beneficiary 
ID), Name, client master or copy of Consolidated 
Account statement, PAN (self-attested scanned copy 
of PAN card), AADHAAR (self-attested scanned copy of 
Aadhaar Card) to secretarial@pngadgil.com . If you are 
an Individual shareholder holding securities in demat 
mode, you are requested to refer to the login method 
explained at step 1 (A) i.e. Login method for e-Voting 
and joining virtual meeting for Individual shareholders 
holding securities in demat mode.
3.  Alternatively, shareholder/members may send a 
request to evoting@nsdl.com  for procuring user 
id and password for e-voting by providing above 
mentioned documents.
4.  In terms of SEBI circular dated December 9, 2020 
on e-Voting facility provided by Listed Companies, 
Individual shareholders holding securities in demat 
mode are allowed to vote through their demat 
account maintained with Depositories and Depository 
Participants. Shareholders are required to update their 
mobile number and email ID correctly in their demat 
account in order to access e-Voting facility.

2. Overview of Financial Performance
 Standalone Performance
  For the financial year ended March 31, 2026, the 
Company recorded a total income of Rs. 1,07,287.10 
million, as compared to Rs. 76,544.25 million in the 
previous financial year, reflecting a growth of 40.16%. 
  The Net Profit for the year under review stood at  
Rs. 4,010.37 million, marking an increase of 86.64 % 
over the Net Profit of Rs. 2,148.78 million reported in the 
previous financial year.
 Consolidated Performance
  For the financial year ended March 31, 2026, the 
Company achieved a total income of Rs. 1,08,271.79 
million, as against Rs. 77,226.19 million in the previous 
financial year, reflecting a growth of 40.20%.
  The Net Profit for the year under review stood at  
Rs. 4,098.20 million, marking an increase of  87.76% 
over the Net Profit of Rs. 2,182.68 million recorded in the 
previous financial year.
3. Share Capital
  During the year under review, the Company has not 
altered its Authorized Share Capital. The Authorized 
Share Capital of the Company is Rs. 2,00,00,00,000 
comprised of 20,00,00,000 Equity Shares of Rs. 10 each.
  The Paid-up capital of the Company as on March 31, 
2026, is Rs. 1,35,70,83,330 comprised of 13,57,08,333 
Equity Shares of Rs. 10 each.
  After the close of the financial year and before the date 
of this Report, the Company successfully completed 
Qualified Institutions Placement (“QIP”) aggregating to 
Rs. 7,000 million. The QIP issue was opened on July 
30, 2026, and closed on August 03, 2026. Accordingly, 
on August 04, 2026, the Company allotted 1,14,94,252 
Equity Shares of face value of Rs. 10 each.
  Consequently, the issued, subscribed and paid-up 
Equity Share capital of the Company increased from Rs. 
135,70,83,330 divided into 13,57,08,333 Equity Shares 
to Rs. 147,20,25,850 divided into 14,72,02,585 Equity 
Shares of Rs. 10 each.
 Further, it is hereby declared that the Company has:
 a.  not issued equity shares with differential rights as 
to dividend, voting or otherwise;
 b. not issued any sweat equity shares;
 c.  not issued employee stock option scheme/plan or 
exercised any option(s)there under; and
 d.  not provided money for purchase of its own 
shares by employees or by trustees for the benefit 
of employees.
4. Dividend
  The Board of Directors of the Company were of the 
opinion that, it would be in the interest of the Company 
to retain earnings for future business requirements and 
business plans. Hence, it was decided to plough back 
the profits of the Company. Accordingly, your Company 
does not recommend dividend on Equity Shares for the 
financial year 2025-26.
  The Dividend Distribution Policy of the Company can 
be accessed at https://www.pngjewellers.com/pages/
investors.

Report

6. Expansion of Retail Footprint
  During the year under review, the Company opened 
25 new stores, taking the total store count to 78 as of 
March 31, 2026. Of these, 57 stores operate under the 
COCO (Company Owned, Company Operated) model 
— comprising 48 Legacy stores and 9 YOOU stores, 
including 1 store in the United States — and 21 stores 
operate under the FOCO (Franchise Owned, Company 
Operated) model, comprising 17 Legacy stores and 4 
YOOU stores.
  Subsequent to the close of the financial year, the 
Company further strengthened its retail presence by 
opening its first Legacy store in the National Capital 
Region (“NCR”) at Gurugram, Haryana, and a YOOU 
format store in Pune, Maharashtra.
  Accordingly, as on the date of this Report, the total store 
count of the Company stands at 80.

17.  Vigil Mechanism / Whistle Blower Policy
  The Company has a Vigil Mechanism/ Whistle 
Blower Policy ( ‘Vigil Mechanism’ ) in place. The Vigil 
Mechanism is a system for providing a tool to the 
Directors and Employees of the Company to report 
violation of personnel policies of the Company, unethical 
behavior, suspected or actual fraud, violation of code of 
conduct. The Company is committed to provide requisite 
safeguards for the protection of the persons who raise 
such concerns from reprisals or victimization.
  The Policy provides for direct access to the Chairperson 
of the Audit Committee in appropriate or exceptional 
cases. The Board of Directors affirm and confirm that no 
employee of the Company has been denied access to 
the Committee.
  Details of the Vigil Mechanism are available on the 
Company’s website www.pngjewellers.com/Investors
18. Risk Management
  The Company has adopted a Risk Management Policy 
in accordance with the provisions of the Companies Act, 
2013 which laid down the framework to identify, evaluate 
business risks and opportunities. The Company has 
vested powers to the Audit Committee to regulate risk 
identification, assessment, analysis and mitigation with 
the assistance of the Internal Auditor. The Company has 
procedures in place for informing the Board of Directors 
on risk assessment and management procedures. 
Senior management periodically reviews this risk 
management framework to keep updated and address 
emerging challenges. The management is however, of 
the view that none of the risks may threaten the existence 
of the Company as a risk mitigation mechanism is put in 
place to ensure that there is nil or minimum impact on 
the Company in case any of these risks materialize.
  The Company has a Business Continuity Plan including 
Disaster Recovery scenario to minimize disruptions 
and potential impact on its employees, customers 
and business during any unforeseen adverse events 
or circumstances.
19.  Policy on Prevention, Prohibition and 
Redressal of Sexual Harassment at 
Workplace
  The Company as required under the provisions of The 
Sexual Harassment of Women at Workplace (Prevention, 
Prohibition and Redressal) Act, 2013 has in place an Anti-
Sexual Harassment Policy in line with the requirements of 
the Act. Internal Complaints Committee (ICC) has been 
set up to redress complaints received regarding sexual 
harassment. All employees (permanent, contractual, 
temporary, trainees) are covered under this policy.
  Following is the summary of sexual harassment 
complaints received and disposed off during the year 
2025-26.
 Number of complaints received during the year: 2
 Number of complaints disposed off during the year: 2
 Number of cases pending for more than ninety days: 0
20.  Adequacy of Internal Financial Controls 
with reference to the Financial 
Statements
  The Company has devised appropriate systems and 
framework for adequate internal financial controls 
with reference to financial statements commensurate 
with the size, scale and complexity of its operations 
including proper delegation of authority, policies and 
procedures, effective IT systems aligned to business 
requirements, risk based internal audit framework and 
risk management framework.
  The Audit Committee regularly reviews the internal 
control system to ensure that it remains effective 
and aligned with the business requirements.  In case 
weaknesses are identified as a result of the reviews, new 
procedures are put in place to strengthen controls.
  Further, the Board annually reviews the effectiveness of 
the Company’s internal control system. The Directors and 
Management confirm that the Internal Financial Controls 
(IFC) are adequate with respect to the operations of 
the Company. 
  A report of the Auditors pursuant to Section 143(3) (i) 
of the Companies Act, 2013 certifying the adequacy 
of Internal Financial Controls is annexed with the 
Auditors’ Report.

26. Credit Rating
 Bank Facilities
  During the year under review, CRISIL reaffirmed the 
Company’s Long-Term Rating at CRISIL A/Stable and 
Short-Term Rating at CRISIL A1. Subsequently, CRISIL 
revised the outlook on the Long-Term Rating from 
‘CRISIL A/Stable’ to ‘CRISIL A/Positive’ while the Short-
Term Rating was reaffirmed at CRISIL A1. 
  Further, India Ratings & Research upgraded the 
Company’s Long-Term Rating for its Bank Loan Facilities 
to IND A+/Stable and affirmed the Short-Term Rating at 
IND A1.
 Fixed Deposits
  CRISIL had revised the outlook on the Company’s Fixed 
Deposit rating from ‘Stable’ to ‘Positive’ and reaffirmed it 
at CRISIL A/Positive.

28.  Conservation of Energy and Technology 
Absorption, Foreign Exchange Earnings 
and Outgo
 I. Conservation of Energy:
   The operations of your Company are not energy 
intensive. However, the Company remains 
committed to making continuous efforts towards 
conservation of energy across its stores and 
office premises. 
  i.  The steps taken or impact on 
conservation of energy;
    The Company has installed a 36 kWp solar 
power plant at its Hadapsar branch location 
Directors’ Report
to utilise renewable energy and reduce 
its dependence on conventional sources 
of electricity.
  ii.  The steps taken by the Company for 
utilizing alternate sources of energy;
    The solar power plant commenced generation 
during the Financial Year 2025-26 and has 
contributed towards meeting a portion of the 
electricity requirements of the branch through 
renewable energy. 
    During the period from January 2026 to March 
2026, the solar plant generated 1,276 units of 
electricity, resulting in an estimated benefit of 
approximately Rs. 25,520, calculated at Rs. 20 
per unit. The initiative reflects the Company’s 
continued efforts towards adoption of 
renewable energy, energy conservation and 
reduction of its environmental footprint.
  iii.  The Capital Investment made energy 
conservation equipment, if any; 
    The Company has incurred a capital 
investment of Rs. 1,23,549/- towards the 
upgradation of the solar power plant, with the 
upgradation work completed in August 2025.
 II.  Technology Absorption, Adaptation and 
Innovation:
   The Company has not carried out any specific 
research and development activities during the year.
 III. Foreign Exchange Earnings and Outgo:
   During the year under review, the Company’s 
foreign exchange earnings in terms of actual inflows 
amounted to Rs. 62.45 million (USD 7,35,600), while

Annual Report on Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR) Activities
[Pursuant to Rule 8 of the Companies (Corporate Social Responsibility Policy) Rules, 2014]
1. Brief outline on CSR Policy of the Company:
  For the Company, CSR not only means Corporate Social Responsibility but also Corporate Social Relationships. It 
is the Company’s commitment to bring an overall positive impact towards sustainable development of the society at 
large. In line with this statement the Company’s CSR Objectives are:
• To enhance value creation in the society through the Company’s CSR initiatives and projects;
• To develop sustainable projects through responsible business practices and good governance;
• To design a process for and ensure an increased commitment at all levels in the organization towards social 
responsibility; and
• To involve its stakeholders and create a support in the process of social transformation.

corporate practices by P N GADGIL JEWELLERS LIMITED (hereinafter referred as “the Company”). Secretarial Audit was 
conducted in a manner that provided us a reasonable basis for evaluating the corporate conducts/statutory compliances 
and expressing our opinion thereon.

maintained by the Company and also the information provided by the Company, its officers, agents and authorised 
representatives during the conduct of secretarial audit, we hereby report that in our opinion, the Company has, during 
the Financial Year ended on March 31, 2026 (the audit period) complied with the statutory provisions listed hereunder and 
also that the Company has proper Board-processes and compliance-mechanism in place to the extent, in the manner and 
subject to the reporting made hereinafter:
We have examined the books, papers, minute books, forms and returns filed, and other records maintained by the Company 
during the audit period according to the provisions of:

3.  We have followed the audit practices and process as were appropriate to obtain reasonable assurance about 
the correctness of the contents of the Secretarial records. The verification was done on test basis to ensure that 
correct facts are reflected in secretarial records. We believe that the processes and practices we followed, provide a 
reasonable basis for our opinion.
4. We have not verified the correctness and appropriateness of financial records and Books of Accounts of the Company.
5.  Wherever required, we have obtained the Management representation about the compliance of laws, rules and 
regulations and happening of events etc.
6.  The Secretarial Audit report is neither an assurance as to the future viability of the Company nor of the efficacy or 
effectiveness with which the management has conducted the affairs of the Company.
